   Images And Words From The Lancash ire Witch Country
Before heading otr to the old vtllage of Pendle Itself, it's certainly worth making Just the slightest of detours Into the
beautlruJ, not to say hlstortc, ctty or Lancaster. Its endlessly twisting backstreets, Vlctortan-style lighting and dark flights
of stairs leading up to ominous-looking buildings straight out of 'THE EXORCIST.'
It's also home to the famous 16th Century castle, where the suspected Witches awaited their grossly unfair trial and
eventual death sentence.
The photo2raph below depicts the impressive main entrance to the castle. The building itself is still very much in use as a
modem-day prison, although, so far as I know, suspected Witches do not feature among the prison's inmates•••


(Below): The awe-inspirlnE view of Pendle Hill from the centre of the picturesque village of Colne. Time really does seem
to stand still here. The authors visited the place on a suitably overcast Sunday afternoon and we were immediately struck
by the caught-breath silence, the sedate pace of country life, and the hard-to-shake feeling that nothing much had changed
here for centuries. The vtllage ts also the birthplace of two convtcted Witches; Kathertne Hewttt (aka ' Moutdheels' ) and
Allce Gray

         N ew S peci es Of Coe l a ca nth Fo u n d I n
                                                       I n do n esi a
Laughing once more in what passes fo r the race of accepted science, a species of nsb said to have been extinct for over 65
million years, has been discovered lying on a cart in an Indonesian fish market.
A series of ge netic tests subsequently revealed that creature, adorned with a se ries of stubby fins, is a member of the
Coelacanth fami ly . It's discovery brings to two the number of species now known to have survived the cataclysmic
disaster that befell the Age Of The Giant Reptiles. Only freshwater lungflsh have similarly survived virtuaJiy unchanged
for over 400 million years.


Back In 1938, scientists ofT the Comoros Islands near South Arrtca, came across a '11v1ng fossil,' and researchers, not
surprisingly, considered the nsh to be a unique relic population. It was beUeved that the creature bad maintained its

 Instead, a lilbot or symbolic representation Is used. In

 to legend, the stone tablets on which God Inscribed the law

For live long hours, the ten-strong team waited                 for
something to happen, and tt wu just cracking on ftrst Ught
when their vigil was finally ' rewarded.'
At a little after 6am, a door In the bar area, which had been
ftrm.ly shut for the whole of the night, suddenly flew open
with no apparent agency.
According to S.E.A's Mike McManus, lt was preceeded by
a whooshlng sound, and as the door crashed open, the
curtains hanging on the window blew up with great force.
As a member approached the middle of the bar, a dark
shadow aped put him heading for the lounge area.
At the same Ume, a smaUlsh shape was seen to dart from
the entrance area Into the lounge. Two members sitting on
the stairs head heard sighing noises which were apparently
picked up on their recording equipment.
As for the buDding Itself, lt has a long history cbtlng back
to 1893. A neighbouring pub, The Rlfteman's Arms, was
demolished years ago. As the decades have rolled by, there
have been reported sightlngs of many ghosts, Including
those of a White Lady, a youn2 boy, an elderly female, a
man In a dark suit and a cellarman.

there was some sort or com.muntcatton between Sadye and the dearly departed Norman, and he was calltng her for a last
farewell.
